   274	        sp.simplify(numer - 2 * rho * (3 + rho**2)) == 0
   275	        and geo_fin == 0
   276	        and r_fin == 0,
   277	    )
   278	
   279	    # Q9 -- closed-form kappa instances by partial sum + tail bracket.
   280	    def series_closed(rr):
   281	        """Closed form of sum_{r>=1} (4r^2+2) rr^r = 2 rr (3+rr^2)/(1-rr)^3."""
   282	        return 2 * rr * (3 + rr**2) / (1 - rr) ** 3
   283	
   284	    ok9 = True
   285	    for rho_v, n_cut, dom_base, series_expect, kappa_expect in (
   286	        (sp.Rational(1, 3), 25, sp.Rational(2, 3),
   287	         sp.Integer(7), sp.Integer(14)),
   288	        (sp.Rational(3, 4), 120, sp.Rational(27, 32),
   289	         sp.Integer(342), sp.Integer(684)),
   290	    ):
   291	        partial = sum(
   292	            (4 * r * r + 2) * rho_v**r for r in range(1, n_cut + 1)
   293	        )
   294	        closed = series_closed(rho_v)
   295	        # tail: for r > n_cut, (4r^2+2) rho^r <= dom_base^r with
   296	        # dom_base < 1 (growth majorant times rho)
   297	        tail_bound = dom_base ** (n_cut + 1) / (1 - dom_base)
   298	        diff = sp.nsimplify(closed - partial)
   299	        if not (diff > 0 and diff < tail_bound):
   300	            ok9 = False
   301	        if sp.simplify(closed - series_expect) != 0:
   302	            ok9 = False
   303	        # kappa = 2 J0 * series (w* carries the |S| = 2 factor)
   304	        if sp.simplify(2 * closed - kappa_expect) != 0:
   305	            ok9 = False
   306	    kappa_1d = 4 * sp.Rational(1, 2) / (1 - sp.Rational(1, 2))
   307	    checks.check(
   308	        "Q9 closed-form kappa instances: kappa_3D = 14 J0 (rho=1/3) and "
   309	        "684 J0 (rho=3/4) certified by partial+tail brackets on the "
   310	        "series; kappa_1D = 4 J0 at rho=1/2",
   311	        ok9 and kappa_1d == 4,
   312	    )
   313	
   314	    # Q10 -- fermionic long-range lift (JW on 4 sites).
   315	    I2 = sp.eye(2)
   316	    ANN = sp.Matrix([[0, 1], [0, 0]])
   317	    SZ = sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [0, -1]])
   318	
   319	    def kron(*mats):
   320	        out = mats[0]
   321	        for m in mats[1:]:
   322	            out = sp.Matrix(sp.kronecker_product(out, m))
   323	        return out
   324	
   325	    def c_op(j, n=4):
   326	        return kron(*([SZ] * j + [ANN] + [I2] * (n - j - 1)))
   327	
   328	    c = [c_op(j) for j in range(4)]
   329	    cd = [m.H for m in c]
   330	
   331	    def com(a, b):
   332	        return a * b - b * a
   333	
   334	    def is_zero(m):
   335	        return sp.simplify(m) == sp.zeros(*m.shape)
   336	
   337	    lr03 = cd[0] * c[3] + cd[3] * c[0]
   338	    hop01 = cd[0] * c[1] + cd[1] * c[0]
   339	    hop12 = cd[1] * c[2] + cd[2] * c[1]
   340	    n0 = cd[0] * c[0]
   341	    n1 = cd[1] * c[1]
   342	    ham_mixed = hop01 + hop12 + lr03
   343	    checks.check(
   344	        "Q10 fermionic long-range lift: distant even pair term is "
   345	        "Hermitian, commutes with intermediate odd/even elements, and "
   346	        "the weighted reduction holds on a mixed family",
   347	        is_zero(lr03 - lr03.H)
   348	        and is_zero(com(lr03, c[1]))
   349	        and is_zero(com(lr03, cd[1]))
   350	        and is_zero(com(lr03, n1))
   351	        and is_zero(com(hop12, n0))
   352	        and is_zero(
   353	            com(ham_mixed, n0) - com(hop01, n0) - com(lr03, n0)
   354	        )
   355	        and not is_zero(com(lr03, n0)),
   356	    )
   357

    47	class CheckRunner:
    48	    def __init__(self):
    49	        self.passed = 0
    50	        self.failed = 0
    51	        self.labels = []
    52	
    53	    def check(self, label, condition):
    54	        ok = bool(condition)
    55	        self.labels.append(label.split()[0])
    56	        if ok:
    57	            self.passed += 1
    58	            print(f"PASS: {label}")
    59	        else:
    60	            self.failed += 1
    61	            print(f"FAIL: {label}")
    62	
    63	    def needle(self, label, path, needles):
    64	        haystack = normalized_whitespace(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    65	        if isinstance(needles, str):
    66	            needles = (needles,)
    67	        self.check(
    68	            label,
    69	            all(normalized_whitespace(n) in haystack for n in needles),
    70	        )
    71	
    72	    def finish(self):
    73	        if self.labels != EXPECTED_LABELS:
    74	            print(
    75	                "FAIL: gate-manifest drift: labels "
    76	                f"{self.labels} != expected {EXPECTED_LABELS}"
    77	            )
    78	            self.failed += 1
    79	        print(f"TOTAL: PASS={self.passed} FAIL={self.failed}")
    80	        return 0 if self.failed == 0 else 1

   211	For `k=1`, (12) gives
   212	
   213	```text
   214	    P_1(x,y) <= J_F F(d(x,y)).                                      (14)
   215	```
   216	
   217	For `k=2`, using (12) and then (3),
   218	
   219	```text
   220	    P_2(x,y)
   221	      <= J_F^2 sum_z F(d(x,z)) F(d(z,y))
   222	      <= J_F^2 C_alpha^{(d)} F(d(x,y)).                            (15)
   223	```
   224	
   225	Inductively, if
   226	
   227	```text
   228	    P_k(x,z) <= J_F^k (C_alpha^{(d)})^(k-1) F(d(x,z)),
   229	```
   230

   232	
   233	```text
   234	    P_{k+1}(x,y)
   235	      = sum_z P_k(x,z) K(z,y)
   236	      <= J_F^(k+1) (C_alpha^{(d)})^(k-1)
   237	         sum_z F(d(x,z)) F(d(z,y))
   238	      <= J_F^(k+1) (C_alpha^{(d)})^k F(d(x,y)).                    (16)
   239	```
   240	
   241	Thus, for every `k >= 1`,
   242	
   243	```text
   244	    P_k(x,y) <= J_F^k (C_alpha^{(d)})^(k-1) F(d(x,y)).              (17)
   245	```
   246	
   247	The reproducing inequality is used once at each induction step.
   248	
   249	### Step 3: Duhamel and the commutator series
   250	
   251	Let
   252	
   253	```text
   254	    A_x(t) = exp(i t H) A_x exp(-i t H).
   255	```
   256	
   257	The finite-dimensional Duhamel expansion of the Heisenberg equation is
   258	
   259	```text
   260	    A_x(t)
   261	      = A_x
   262	        + sum_{k>=1} (i t)^k / k! ad_H^k(A_x),
   263	    ad_H(O) := [H,O].                                               (18)
   264	```
   265	
   266	Since `x != y` implies `[A_x,B_y]=0`, the first nonzero contribution to
   267	`[A_x(t),B_y]` is controlled by interaction chains that connect `x` to `y`. Each commutator with a local term contributes the elementary bound
   268	
   269	```text
   270	    ||[h_Z,O]|| <= 2 ||h_Z|| ||O||.                                 (19)
   271	```
   272	
   273	Applying (19) along a length-`k` interaction chain and summing over all intermediate sites gives the term-by-term estimate
   274	
   275	```text
   276	    T_k
   277	      := || k-th Duhamel contribution to [A_x(t),B_y] ||
   278	      <= 2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| (2 |t|)^k P_k(x,y) / k!.                 (20)
   279	```
   280	
   281	Insert (17) into (20):
   282	
   283	```text
   284	    T_k
   285	      <= 2 ||A_x|| ||B_y||
   286	         (2 |t|)^k J_F^k (C_alpha^{(d)})^(k-1) F(d(x,y)) / k!
   287	      = (2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| / C_alpha^{(d)})
   288	        (2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F |t|)^k F(d(x,y)) / k!.                 (21)
   289	```
   290	
   291	Summing (21) for `k >= 1` yields
   292	
   293	```text
   294	    ||[A_x(t),B_y]||
   295	      <= (2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| / C_alpha^{(d)})
   296	         sum_{k>=1} (2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F |t|)^k / k!
   297	         F(d(x,y))
   298	      = (2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| / C_alpha^{(d)})
   299	        (exp(2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F |t|) - 1)
   300	        F(d(x,y)).                                                  (22)
   301	```
   302	
   303	This proves (L3).
   304	
   305	### Step 4: finite velocity
   306	
   307	For any `0 < mu' < mu`,
   308	
   309	```text
   310	    F_{mu,alpha}(r)
   311	      = exp(-mu' r) exp(-(mu-mu')r) (1+r)^(-alpha)
   312	      <= exp(-mu' r).                                               (23)
   313	```
   314	
   315	Substituting (23) into (22) gives
   316	
   317	```text
   318	    ||[A_x(t),B_y]||
   319	      <= (2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| / C_alpha^{(d)})
   320	         exp(2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F |t| - mu' d(x,y)).                 (24)
   321	```
   322	
   323	The exponent is nonpositive outside
   324	
   325	```text
   326	    d(x,y) >= (2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F / mu') |t|,
   327	```
   328	
   329	which proves the finite velocity (5).

   341	- The Hastings-Koma reproducing-weight convention for polynomially corrected exponentials; the constant used here is derived in Step 1 rather than imported.
   342	- The companion runner's finite-block log-transfer example is a numerical witness for one dynamics instance, not a theorem that all such transfer logarithms are quasilocal.
   343	
   344	## What this note does NOT claim
   345	
   346	- It does not claim a pure exponential convolution constant on `Z^d`; (L1) proves that such a constant is unavailable.
   347	- It does not add a new axiom or a new primitive dynamics.
   348	- It does not prove that every strictly local positive transfer operator has an exponentially decaying logarithm.
   349	- It does not assert any audit status for this note or for its consumers.
   350	- It does not provide phenomenological values, literature numerical comparators, or phenomenological constants.
   351

     2	
     3	**Date:** 2026-06-11
     4	**Type:** bounded_theorem
     5	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
     6	**Claim scope:** This note proves a finite-dimensional Lieb-Robinson bound for Hamiltonians on finite blocks `Lambda subset Z^d` whose interaction family has finite Hastings-Koma weighted norm for `F_{mu,alpha}(r) = exp(-mu r)/(1+r)^alpha`, with fixed `d` and `alpha > d`.     12	
    13	## Why this note exists
    14	
    15	The retained finite-range Lieb-Robinson lane is adequate for Hamiltonians whose interaction supports have a fixed diameter. It is not adequate for exact logarithms of strictly local transfer operators after noncommuting factors are multiplied: BCH commutators generate longer supports with exponentially small coefficients.
    16	
    17	The tempting repair is to put a pure exponential weight `G_mu(r) = exp(-mu r)` into the finite-range proof. That fails on `Z^d`. If `x = 0`, `y = R e_1`, and `z = k e_1` runs along the geodesic from `x` to `y`, then
    18	
    19	```text
    20	    G_mu(d(x,z)) G_mu(d(z,y)) / G_mu(d(x,y))
    21	      = exp[-mu(k + R-k - R)]
    22	      = 1
    23	```
    24	
    25	for each `k = 0,...,R`. Thus
    26	
    27	```text
    28	    sum_z G_mu(d(x,z)) G_mu(d(z,y)) / G_mu(d(x,y))  >=  R + 1,
    29	```
    30	
    31	so no volume-independent convolution constant exists for the pure exponential weight. This note uses the standard reproducing weight with a polynomial denominator and displays the finite constant.
    32	
    33	## Setting
    34	
    35	Let `Lambda` be a finite subset of `Z^d` with graph metric `d(x,y) = ||x-y||_1`. The site Hilbert space is finite dimensional. A Hamiltonian is written as
    36	
    37	```text
    38	    H = sum_{Z subset Lambda} h_Z,
    39	```
    40	
    41	where each `h_Z = h_Z^*` acts trivially outside `Z`, and the sum is finite. For `mu > 0` and `alpha > d`, define
    42	
    43	```text
    44	    F_{mu,alpha}(r) = exp(-mu r) / (1+r)^alpha.
    45	```
    46	
    47	This note uses the pair weighted interaction norm
    48	
    49	```text
    50	    ||H||_{mu,alpha}
    51	      := sup_{u,v in Lambda}
    52	            F_{mu,alpha}(d(u,v))^{-1}
    53	            sum_{Z contains u,v} ||h_Z||.                         (1)
    54	```
    55	
    56	This is the clean interaction norm for the proof below. The more local diameter version
    57	
    58	```text
    59	    sup_u sum_{Z contains u} ||h_Z|| / F_{mu,alpha}(diam Z)
    60	```
    61	
    62	implies finiteness of (1), because `d(u,v) <= diam Z` for every `u,v in Z` and `F_{mu,alpha}` is decreasing.
    63	
    64	Set
    65	
    66	```text
    67	    S_alpha^{(d)} := sum_{z in Z^d} (1+||z||_1)^(-alpha)  < infinity,
    68	    C_alpha^{(d)} := 2^alpha S_alpha^{(d)}.                       (2)
    69	```
    70	
    71	The runner computes finite-box approximations to this constant in `d=1` and `d=2` and checks the reproducing inequality numerically.
    72	
    73	## Statements
    74	
    75	**(L1) Pure-exponential obstruction.** For `G_mu(r)=exp(-mu r)`, the convolution ratio
    76	
    77	```text
    78	    R_G(x,y) := sum_z G_mu(d(x,z)) G_mu(d(z,y)) / G_mu(d(x,y))
    79	```
    80	
    81	is unbounded on `Z^d`. Already in `d=1`, `R_G(0,R) >= R+1`.
    82	
    83	**(L2) Reproducing weight.** For `F = F_{mu,alpha}` with `alpha > d`,
    84	
    85	```text
    86	    sum_{z in Z^d} F(d(x,z)) F(d(z,y))
    87	      <= C_alpha^{(d)} F(d(x,y))                                  (3)
    88	```
    89	
    90	with the explicit finite constant (2).
    91	
    92	**(L3) Quasilocal Lieb-Robinson bound.** Let `J_F := ||H||_{mu,alpha}` from (1). For single-site observables `A_x` and `B_y`,
    93	
    94	```text
    95	    ||[ exp(i t H) A_x exp(-i t H), B_y ]||
    96	      <= (2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| / C_alpha^{(d)})
    97	         ( exp(2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F |t|) - 1 )
    98	         F_{mu,alpha}(d(x,y)).                                    (4)
    99	```
   100	
   101	Every constant in (4) is finite on finite `Lambda` whenever `J_F < infinity`.
   102	
   103	**(L4) Finite velocity after a rate split.** For any `0 < mu' < mu`,
   104	
   105	```text
   106	    F_{mu,alpha}(r) <= exp(-mu' r),
   107	```
   108	
   109	and therefore (4) gives the cone form
   110	
   111	```text
   112	    ||[A_x(t),B_y]||
   113	      <= (2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| / C_alpha^{(d)})
   114	         exp(2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F |t| - mu' d(x,y)).
   115	```
   116	
   117	The associated finite velocity is
   118	
   119	```text
   120	    v(mu') = 2 C_alpha^{(d)} J_F / mu'.                            (5)
   121	```
   122	
   123	**(L5) Finite-range consistency.** If the interaction is finite range, then `J_F` is finite for every `mu > 0` and `alpha > d`, so (4) specializes to a valid finite-range Lieb-Robinson envelope. Its spatial factor is no slower than the pure exponential `exp(-mu r)`, because `(1+r)^(-alpha) <= 1`.
   124	
   125	**(L6) Conditional consumer corollary for log-transfer dynamics.** If a downstream log-transfer source supplies finite `J_F = ||H_log||_{mu,alpha}` for some `mu > 0` and `alpha > 3`, then (4) applies on the framework's finite `Z^3` blocks. This is not stated as an axiom-level derivation for every transfer operator: the companion runner constructs one concrete strictly local positive transfer
   126	
   127	```text
   128	    T = exp(-A/2) exp(-B) exp(-A/2)
   129	```
   130	
   131	with noncommuting nearest-neighbor qubit terms, computes `H_log = -log T`, decomposes it in the Pauli basis, verifies finite `J_F`, and checks the bound without fitted constants.
   132	
   133	## Proof
   134	
   135	### Step 1: the reproducing inequality
   136	
   137	Write
   138	
   139	```text
   140	    r = d(x,y),    a = d(x,z),    b = d(z,y).
   141	```
   142	
   143	The triangle inequality gives `r <= a+b`, hence
   144	
   145	```text
   146	    exp[-mu(a+b-r)] <= 1.                                          (6)
   147	```
   148	
   149	Now compute the pointwise ratio:
   150	
   151	```text
   152	    F(a)F(b)/F(r)
   153	      = exp[-mu(a+b-r)] (1+r)^alpha / ((1+a)^alpha (1+b)^alpha).   (7)
   154	```
   155	
   156	Since `1+r <= 1+a+b <= (1+a)+(1+b)` and, for positive `U,V`,
   157	
   158	```text
   159	    (U+V)^alpha <= 2^(alpha-1)(U^alpha + V^alpha),
   160	```
   161	
   162	we have
   163	
   164	```text
   165	    (1+r)^alpha
   166	      <= 2^(alpha-1)((1+a)^alpha + (1+b)^alpha).                   (8)
   167	```
   168	
   169	Combining (6), (7), and (8),
   170	
   171	```text
   172	    F(a)F(b)/F(r)
   173	      <= 2^(alpha-1)
   174	         ( (1+a)^(-alpha) + (1+b)^(-alpha) ).                      (9)
   175	```
   176	
   177	Summing over `z in Z^d` gives
   178	
   179	```text
   180	    sum_z F(d(x,z)) F(d(z,y)) / F(d(x,y))
   181	      <= 2^(alpha-1) sum_z (1+d(x,z))^(-alpha)
   182	       + 2^(alpha-1) sum_z (1+d(z,y))^(-alpha)
   183	      = 2^alpha sum_z (1+||z||_1)^(-alpha)
   184	      = C_alpha^{(d)}.                                             (10)
   185	```
   186	
   187	Because `alpha > d`, the lattice sum in (10) converges. This proves (L2).
   188	
   189	### Step 2: interaction kernel bound
   190	
   191	Define
   192	
   193	```text
   194	    K(u,v) := sum_{Z contains u,v} ||h_Z||.                         (11)
   195	```
   196	
   197	By (1),
   198	
   199	```text
   200	    K(u,v) <= J_F F(d(u,v)).                                        (12)
   201	```
   202	
   203	For `k >= 1`, define the weighted path sums
   204	
   205	```text
   206	    P_1(x,y) := K(x,y),
   207	    P_k(x,y) := sum_{z_1,...,z_{k-1}}
   208	                K(x,z_1) K(z_1,z_2) ... K(z_{k-1},y).              (13)
   209	```
   210

     1	# Free Bilinear Exact-Log Quasilocal Lieb-Robinson Bridge
     2	
     3	**Date:** 2026-06-10
     4	**Type:** bounded_theorem
     5	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
     6	**Claim scope:** in the free (`U = 1`) bilinear staggered two-step sector,
     7	the exact reconstructed Hamiltonian
     8	`H = -log(T_hat^2)/(2 a_tau)` whose kernel is supplied by
     9	[`TRANSFER_MATRIX_LOG_QUASILOCALITY_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md`](TRANSFER_MATRIX_LOG_QUASILOCALITY_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md)
    10	obeys a finite-velocity quasilocal Lieb-Robinson envelope. If
    11	`0 < d mu < eta < arcsinh(m)` and
    12	`W_mu := sup_x sum_y ||Phi_{xy}|| exp(mu d_1(x,y))`, then
    13	`W_mu < infinity` and, for one-site observables,
    14	
    15	```text
    16	    ||[alpha_t(A_x), B_y]||
    17	      <= 2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| exp(-mu d_1(x,y) + 4 W_mu |t|).
    18	```
    19	
    20	Equivalently the finite lightcone speed at this `mu` is
    21	`v_mu = 4 W_mu / mu`. The strict finite-range `R <= 2` exact-log claim
    22	remains false on this sector; the gauged/interacting exact-log locality and
    23	full continuum microcausality are not claimed here.
    24	
    25	**Status authority:** independent audit lane only. This source note does not
    26	set or predict audit status.
    27	
    28	**Primary runner:** [`scripts/free_bilinear_quasilocal_lr_bridge_2026_06_10.py`](../scripts/free_bilinear_quasilocal_lr_bridge_2026_06_10.py)
    29	**Cache:** [`logs/runner-cache/free_bilinear_quasilocal_lr_bridge_2026_06_10.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/free_bilinear_quasilocal_lr_bridge_2026_06_10.txt)
    30	
    31	## Why this note exists
    32	
    33	The parent microcausality row asked for a finite-range or quasilocal
    34	decomposition of the exact reconstructed logarithmic Hamiltonian
    35	`H = -log(T)/a_tau`, and for the parent LR constant to be updated from the
    36	stale `2 e r J` form to the overlap-weight convention of
    37	[`MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md).
    38	The transfer-matrix log-quasilocality theorem supplies the missing exact-log kernel
    39	and exponential tail, but deliberately leaves the quasilocal
    40	Lieb-Robinson composition as a named next theorem. This note supplies exactly
    41	that composition, still only on the free bilinear sector where the kernel is
    42	currently proved.
    43	
    44	## Inputs
    45	
    46	1. **Exact-log kernel.** The transfer-matrix log-quasilocality theorem proves that,
    47	   for the free bilinear two-step transfer matrix,
    48	
    49	   ```text
    50	       H = sum_{x,y} h(x-y) a_x^dag a_y,
    51	       |h(z)| <= (1/a_tau) C_d(eta,m) exp(-eta ||z||_inf)
    52	   ```
    53	
    54	   for every `0 < eta < eta* := arcsinh(m)`, with explicit
    55	   `C_d(eta,m) = sqrt(m^2 + (d-1) + cosh^2 eta)`, and translates this into a
    56	   support-family kernel with finite unweighted per-site overlap
    57	   `W_H = ||h||_1`.
    58	2. **Overlap-weight LR convention.** The finite-range microcausality bridge
    59	   proves the support-family LR lemma in the overlap-weight variables
    60	   `(q, R, W)` and supersedes the parent row's stale `2 e r J` constant.
    61	   This note reuses that path-counting convention but performs the
    62	   exponentially weighted quasilocal path sum directly.
    63	3. **One-site algebra.** The current
    64	   [`MINIMAL_AXIOMS_2026-06-29.md`](MINIMAL_AXIOMS_2026-06-29.md) supplies the
    65	   finite one-qubit one-site operator algebra. No record, readout, species,
    66	   gauge, or continuum bridge is consumed.
    67	
    68	No empirical value, fitted parameter, PDG input, or observed comparator enters
    69	the proof.
    70	
    71	## Statement
    72	
    73	Let `Phi_{xy}` denote the two-site hopping term associated with the exact-log
    74	kernel and write `d_1(x,y)` for the lattice `l1` distance. For any
    75	`mu > 0` define
    76	
    77	```text
    78	    W_mu := sup_x sum_y ||Phi_{xy}|| exp(mu d_1(x,y)).
    79	```
    80	
    81	**(B1) Finite weighted overlap.** If `0 < d mu < eta < arcsinh(m)`, then
    82	`W_mu` is finite. Indeed, from the cited kernel bound and
    83	`||z||_1 <= d ||z||_inf`,
    84	
    85	```text
    86	    W_mu <= (C_d(eta,m)/a_tau)
    87	             sum_{r>=0} [(2r+1)^d - (2r-1)^d] exp(-(eta - d mu) r),
    88	```
    89	
    90	where the `r=0` shell is read as one site. The right-hand side converges
    91	because `eta - d mu > 0`.
    92	
    93	**(B2) Weighted-path LR bound.** For one-site observables `A_x`, `B_y` and
    94	`alpha_t(A) = exp(i t H) A exp(-i t H)`,
    95	
    96	```text
    97	    ||[alpha_t(A_x), B_y]||
    98	      <= 2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| exp(-mu d_1(x,y) + 4 W_mu |t|).              (1)
    99	```
   100	
   101	Thus the sector has a finite quasilocal lightcone speed
   102	`v_mu = 4 W_mu / mu`.
   103	
   104	**(B3) Sector boundary.** This is not the strict finite-range parent
   105	hypothesis. The exact log Hamiltonian on this sector has nonzero range-4
   106	hops, so it must be read as quasilocal. The result is also not a theorem for
   107	fixed-background gauge fields, interacting transfer matrices, or the
   108	`m = 0` gapless boundary.
   109	
   110	## Proof
   111	
   112	### Step 1: weighted norm closure
   113	
   114	The transfer-matrix log-quasilocality theorem gives
   115	`|h(z)| <= C exp(-eta ||z||_inf)`. Since `||z||_1 <= d ||z||_inf`,
   116	
   117	```text
   118	    |h(z)| exp(mu ||z||_1)
   119	      <= C exp(-(eta - d mu) ||z||_inf).
   120	```
   121	
   122	Summing over `l_inf` shells gives (B1). This is the only place where the
   123	strict inequality `d mu < eta` is used.
   124	
   125	### Step 2: weighted path expansion
   126	
   127	Expand the Heisenberg evolution in nested commutators:
   128	
   129	```text
   130	    alpha_t(A_x) = sum_{n>=0} (i t)^n ad_H^n(A_x) / n!.
   131	```
   132	
   133	A nonzero contribution to `[ad_H^n(A_x), B_y]` is a chain of pair terms whose
   134	supports connect `x` to `y`. Each commutator contributes a harmless factor
   135	`2`, and the final commutator with `B_y` contributes another factor `2`.
   136	For any path `x = x_0, x_1, ..., x_n = y`,
   137	
   138	```text
   139	    prod_j ||Phi_{x_{j-1} x_j}||
   140	      <= exp(-mu d_1(x,y))
   141	         prod_j (||Phi_{x_{j-1} x_j}|| exp(mu d_1(x_{j-1},x_j))),
   142	```
   143	
   144	by the triangle inequality. Summing over intermediate sites bounds the
   145	weighted path sum by `exp(-mu d_1(x,y)) W_mu^n`. Therefore
   146	
   147	```text
   148	    ||[alpha_t(A_x), B_y]||
   149	      <= 2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| exp(-mu d_1(x,y))
   150	         sum_{n>=1} (2 W_mu |t|)^n / n!.
   151	```
   152	
   153	Since `sum_{n>=1} u^n/n! <= exp(u)` and the bridge is intentionally
   154	conservative, we record the weaker but simpler envelope (1) with
   155	`4 W_mu |t|`. This proves (B2).
   156	
   157	### Step 3: finite-speed reading
   158	
   159	Equation (1) can be written
   160	
   161	```text
   162	    2 ||A_x|| ||B_y|| exp[-mu (d_1(x,y) - v_mu |t|)],
   163	    v_mu = 4 W_mu / mu.
   164	```
   165	
   166	For fixed `mu`, this is a finite lattice lightcone. The constants are not
   167	claimed optimal; their purpose is to close the existence and finite-speed
   168	composition step from the cited exact-log kernel.
   169	
   170	## Runner checks
   171	
   172	The runner performs five checks:
   173	
   174	1. computes `W_mu` from the cited `d = 1` exact-log kernel and verifies it
   175	   is below the cited strip-bound shell sum;
   176	2. computes a `Z^3` weighted overlap on a `64^3` kernel and verifies it is
   177	   below the same shell-sum upper bound when `3 mu < eta`;
   178	3. directly checks the weighted convolution path inequality on sampled
   179	   path lengths;
   180	4. builds a finite hard-core bilinear matrix Hamiltonian from the exact-log
   181	   kernel and checks the commutator envelope against exact matrix evolution;
   182	5. falsifies the theorem against a positive, gapped symbol with an algebraic
   183	   Fourier tail, whose exponential weighted norm grows with cutoff.
   184	
   185	Expected output:
   186	
   187	```text
   188	TOTAL: PASS=5 FAIL=0
   189	```
   190	
   191	## Boundaries
   192	
   193	- **Free bilinear only.** The theorem consumes the translation-invariant
   194	  exact-log kernel of the free two-step sector. It does not prove
   195	  gauged/interacting log-transfer locality.
   196	- **Mass gap required.** At `m = 0`, the transfer-matrix log-quasilocality theorem
   197	  records power-law tails; then no positive `eta` is available and this
   198	  bridge does not apply.
   199	- **Continuum microcausality not automatic.** The theorem supplies a finite
   200	  lattice lightcone. Any continuum spacelike-commutator statement must still
   201	  cite a matching Lorentz scaling bridge and keep its sector assumptions
   202	  explicit.
   203	- **No new axiom.** This is a theorem-level consequence of the cited
   204	  exact-log kernel and the current one-site algebra; it adds no primitive.

    11	
    12	# Microcausality: Fermionic Even-CAR Walk-Expansion Lieb-Robinson Bound
    13	
    14	**Date:** 2026-07-18
    15	**Type:** bounded_theorem
    16	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
    17	**Scope:** bridge-conditional; supplied fermionic class (CAR algebra,
    18	even Hermitian bond terms, finite region); the axioms supply no
    19	dynamics and no fermionic carrier; same conventions and declared ODE
    20	context as the sibling chain.
    21	**Audit-status authority:** independent audit lane only. This note sets
    22	no audit verdict and predicts none.
    23	**Primitive status:** no primitive is approved, registered, edited, or
    24	enlarged here.
    25	**Primary runner:**
    26	[`scripts/microcausality_fermionic_even_car_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py`](../scripts/microcausality_fermionic_even_car_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py)
    27	**Runner cache:**
    28	[`logs/runner-cache/microcausality_fermionic_even_car_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/microcausality_fermionic_even_car_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt)
    29	
    30	## Purpose
    31	
    32	The sibling
    33	[`MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_ALL_TIME_VOLUME_UNIFORM_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md)
    34	closes the family's walk-expansion task for qubit bond Hamiltonians and
    35	names, in its Non-Claims, "the fermionic transfer bridge" as open. That
    36	bridge has two halves: (i) extend the Lieb-Robinson chain to a
    37	**supplied CAR algebra** (no identification of this algebra with any
    38	framework matter sector is made or needed here), and (ii) identify a
    39	transfer-operator-generated Hamiltonian to which it applies. This note supplies half (i) only. Half (ii)
    40	(Berezin/log-transfer identification) is **not attempted** and remains
    41	open.
    42	
    43	The extension is not a representation transfer. An allowed even bond
    44	term need not stay two-factor-local under Jordan-Wigner: terms whose
    45	local parity at each endpoint is odd — hopping and pairing — acquire a
    46	`Z`-string through the JW-intermediate sites when the two sites are
    47	not adjacent in the chosen JW order (terms with even local parity at
    48	each endpoint, such as `n_i n_j`, stay two-factor-local; the
    49	obstruction is endpoint-parity-dependent, which is exactly why a
    50	uniform representation transfer is unavailable). The runner gates this
    51	concretely: the JW image of the hop between JW-nonadjacent sites fails
    52	to commute with an intermediate qubit operator (it is not supported on
    53	the two qubit factors), while CAR locality holds (it commutes with the
    54	odd intermediate generators `c_2, c_2^†` and the even intermediate
    55	element `n_2`). So the qubit-bond
    56	theorem does not apply as stated, and the correct replacement is an
    57	intrinsic **graded locality lemma** on the CAR algebra — the one new
    58	load-bearing ingredient here. The lemma is **proof-carried** (its short
    59	induction is written in full below) and supported by an exhaustive
    60	exact gate: the runner verifies the graded commutation sign
    61	`(−1)^{p·q}` over the complete homogeneous monomial basis of two
    62	disjoint two-site regions (256 pairs), including an explicit
    63	discrimination clause showing the exponent `p·q` — not `p + q` — is
    64	forced. With the lemma, every **post-initial-term** step of the
    65	sibling chain carries over; the initial-term deletion (`[A, B] = 0`)
    66	carries only when at least one observable is even, and the theorem
    67	below keeps the zeroth term explicitly in the general case.
    68	
    69	## Hypotheses (all supplied, none derived)
    70	
    71	A finite region `Λ ⊂ Z^3` with induced nearest-neighbor bond set
    72	`E(Λ)` (assumed nonempty; at `E(Λ) = ∅` set `J = 0`, `H = 0`, and every
    73	statement is trivial); the CAR algebra `CAR(Λ)` with generators
    74	`c_x, c_x^†` (`x ∈ Λ`)
    75	and relations `{c_x, c_y^†} = δ_{xy}`, `{c_x, c_y} = {c_x^†, c_y^†} =
    76	0`; for `S ⊆ Λ`, `CAR(S)` is the subalgebra generated by the `c_x,
    77	c_x^†` with `x ∈ S`, and an element is **even** (resp. **odd**) when it
    78	is a sum of monomials with an even (resp. odd) number of generators. A
    79	supplied bond Hamiltonian `H = Σ_{b∈E(Λ)} h_b` with each `h_b` **even**,
    80	Hermitian, in `CAR(b)`, and `J = max_b ||h_b||` (evenness is
    81	load-bearing: the graded lemma requires it, and the runner exhibits an
    82	odd term breaking the reduction step). Observables `A ∈ CAR(X)`,
    83	`B ∈ CAR(Y)` of **arbitrary parity**, with `X ∩ Y = ∅`, equivalently
    84	`d ≥ 1` — required throughout, as a **scoping hypothesis**: it gives
    85	the series start `k ≥ d` its content (the reach lemma) and matches the
    86	sibling's clean-form scope. Stated precisely: the sibling's gated
    87	`d = 0` counterexample refutes the **clean** form (no zeroth term);
    88	the general-parity form below keeps `||[A, B]||` explicitly and is not
    89	refuted by that instance — the `d = 0` case is simply **out of scope**
    90	here, not claimed false. `d = d(X, Y)` is the `Z^3` graph distance; `n_X = #{b ∈ E(Λ) : b ∩ X ≠ ∅} ≤ 6·|X|`;
    91	Heisenberg convention `τ_t(A) = e^{itH} A e^{−itH}`.
    92	
    93	Computation and norms: the finite CAR algebra is realized faithfully by
    94	the Jordan-Wigner construction `c_j = (Π_{k<j} Z_k) σ^-_j` (declared
    95	context; the CAR relations are gated in the representation, and the
    96	operator norm is representation-level matrix analysis). The declared
    97	finite-matrix ODE context of the sibling (exponential calculus,
    98	time-ordered propagator existence, Riemann limit passage) is reused
    99	unchanged, including the directed-time discipline: bounds are proved
   100	for `t ≥ 0` and extended to `t < 0` by the `H → −H` symmetry, under
   101	which the even Hermitian bond class is invariant with the same `J`,
   102	bonds, and walks. The axioms supply no dynamics (needled) and no
   103	fermionic carrier (the axiom memo's four-axiom surface contains no
   104	fermion axiom; the CAR algebra enters only as a supplied object here);
   105	everything is bridge-conditional exactly as in the siblings. No literature statement is load-bearing; the
   106	Lieb-Robinson line for lattice fermions is a comparator class only.
   107	
   108	## Results
   109	
   110	**Graded locality lemma (rebuilt from the CAR relations; local alias
   111	L-F).** This lemma is **proof-carried**: the following induction is the
   112	proof, and the runner supports it with an exhaustive exact gate rather
   113	than replacing it. Let `S ∩ T = ∅`, let `A ∈ CAR(S)` be a monomial of
   114	`p` generators and `B ∈ CAR(T)` a monomial of `q` generators.
   115	Cross-site generators anticommute: for `x ≠ y`, every pair among
   116	`c_x, c_x^†, c_y, c_y^†` taken across the two sites anticommutes
   117	(`δ_{xy} = 0` in the relations; gated at the generator level). Moving
   118	one generator of `B` across all of `A` therefore gives the sign
   119	`(−1)^p`; iterating over `B`'s `q` generators,
   120	
   121	> `A·B = (−1)^{p·q} B·A`.
   122	
   123	By bilinearity: **even `A` commutes with every disjoint `B`** (any
   124	parity), and **odd-odd disjoint pairs anticommute** (so their
   125	commutator is generically nonzero — gated with a Majorana pair). The
   126	graded table is gated (runner group C), the full sign law is gated
   127	exhaustively over the complete homogeneous monomial basis of two
   128	disjoint two-site regions — 256 ordered pairs, with a discrimination
   129	clause proving the exponent is `p·q` and **not** `p + q` (the mixed
   130	even-odd pairs separate the two laws) — and the odd-term necessity
   131	exhibit (gate C5) shows the reduction step below genuinely fails
   132	without evenness.
   133	
   134	**Motivation exhibit (JW strings; why L-F is needed).** On four sites
   135	with JW order `0 < 1 < 2 < 3`, the even hop `h = c_1^† c_3 + c_3^† c_1`
   136	between JW-nonadjacent sites has JW image carrying `Z_2`: it fails to
   137	commute with the qubit operator `X_2` (gated), so it is **not** a qubit
   138	bond term on the factors `{1, 3}` — the sibling's hypothesis class does
   139	not contain it representation-wise. Its CAR locality is nevertheless
   140	exact: it commutes with the odd site-2 generators `c_2, c_2^†` and
   141	with the even site-2 element `n_2` (gated) — precisely the graded
   142	lemma. So the fermionic theorem is not a corollary of the qubit
   143	theorem by JW transfer; it needs the graded argument.
   144	
   145	**Chain carry-over (every post-initial disjointness step replaced by
   146	the graded lemma).** The sibling chain uses locality in exactly three
   147	places: the boundary reduction, the base-term vanishing, and the
   148	initial-term deletion `[A, B] = 0`. The graded lemma supplies the
   149	first two for even bond terms against arbitrary-parity observables;
   150	the third it supplies only when at least one observable is even, which
   151	is why the theorem keeps the zeroth term in the general case:
   152	
   153	1. *Boundary reduction:* `[H, O] = [H_{∂Z}, O]` for `O ∈ CAR(Z)`, since
   154	   each `h_b` with `b ∩ Z = ∅` is even and disjoint (L-F; gated at the
   155	   fermionic instance `[H, n_0] = [hop_{01}, n_0]` on the four-site
   156	   chain, with the two far hops commuting exactly). The self-drop
   157	   `[h_b, h_b] = 0` and the per-bond re-derivation with the reduced
   158	   generator `H̃_b` (self term dropped **before** the Jacobi step) are
   159	   as in the sibling (gated).
   160	2. *Base-term vanishing:* `[h_{b_k}, B] = 0` unless `b_k ∩ Y ≠ ∅`
   161	   (`h_{b_k}` even and disjoint from `Y`; L-F — same gate family), and
   162	   `[A, B] = 0` at `d ≥ 1` for arbitrary parities? **No** — this is
   163	   the one place arbitrary parity matters: for odd `A` and odd `B`
   164	   disjoint, `[A, B] = 2AB ≠ 0` in general. The theorem's series
   165	   nevertheless starts at `k ≥ d ≥ 1` with **no** `||[A, B]||` term
   166	   only when that term vanishes; for odd-odd pairs it does not, and
   167	   the honest statement keeps it: the bound proved is
   168	
   169	   > `||[τ_t(A), B]|| ≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X/10)
   170	   > Σ_{k≥d} (20J|t|)^k / k!`,
   171	
   172	   with `||[A, B]|| = 0` whenever `A` or `B` is even (L-F), i.e. the
   173	   sibling's clean form holds for the even-observable sector, and the
   174	   general-parity form carries the exact `t = 0` commutator as its
   175	   zeroth term (which any correct bound must, since `τ_0 = id`). Both
   176	   cases are gated (even probe: clean; odd-odd: `t = 0` term nonzero,
   177	   exhibited).
   178	
   179	All remaining steps of the sibling chain are algebra-independent matrix
   180	analysis or `Z^3` geometry, unchanged: Jacobi and
   181	conjugation-distribution, the directed-time norm-transport lemma and
   182	`H → −H` extension, the commutator norm bound, iterated integrals
   183	`|t|^k/k!`, the walk combinatorics (bonds per site `6`, bond-adjacency
   184	degree `10`, `|𝒲_k| ≤ n_X·10^{k−1}`, reach `k ≥ d`), the coefficient
   185	assembly `(2J)^k n_X 10^{k−1} = (n_X/10)(20J)^k` (re-gated here), the
   186	factorial tail lemma, the `μ`-reweighted exponential form, and the
   187	vanishing remainder. They are cited to the sibling where they are
   188	natively gated; this note re-gates the algebra-adjacent instances in
   189	the fermionic representation:
   190	
   191	- below-cone vanishing at `d = 3` for `k = 0, 1, 2` against **both** an
   192	  even probe (`n_3`) and an odd probe (`c_3 + c_3^†`) — the graded
   193	  lemma in action, since the evolved observable here is even and L-F
   194	  kills both parities of disjoint probe;
   195	- cone arrival at `k = d = 3` against both probes (the family's
   196	  reaching-instance discipline);
   197	- parity preservation of the adjoint chain for even `H` (exhibit);
   198	- Hermiticity of generator sums (`H̃` self-adjoint, as G2 requires);
   199	- an even **pairing** term `c_x^† c_y^† + c_y c_x` alongside hopping
   200	  (Hermitian, even, disjoint-commuting — the class is not
   201	  hopping-only; gated).
   202	
   203	**Theorem (fermionic all-time volume-uniform Lieb-Robinson bound).**
   204	For the supplied even-CAR class above with `d ≥ 1`, for all `t` and
   205	every finite `Λ`:
   206	
   207	> `||[τ_t(A), B]|| ≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X/10)
   208	> Σ_{k≥d} (20J|t|)^k / k!`
   209	> `≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X/10) · ((20J|t|)^d/d!) · e^{20J|t|}`,
   210	
   211	with `||[A, B]|| = 0` whenever `A` or `B` is even. Constants depend
   212	only on `||A||`, `||B||`, `n_X ≤ 6|X|`, `J`, `d` — not on `|Λ|`. The
   213	`μ`-reweighted exponential tail form and the `μ = 1` velocity-type
   214	readout `20eJ` carry over verbatim (the tail factor is
   215	algebra-independent) — with the precise reading that for odd-odd
   216	pairs they control the **dynamical tail** (the series term) only: the
   217	zeroth term `||[A, B]||` is `t`- and distance-independent and is not
   218	claimed to decay. Neither `20J` (walk-series activity scale) nor
   219	`20eJ` is claimed sharp.
   220	
   221	## No-Go Discipline Gate
   222

   224	  routes).** The three residuals this note does NOT take, with why
   225	  each is neither smuggled nor foreclosed: (i) transfer-operator

   233	  (iii) `U`-integrated statement — NOT ATTEMPTED: no gauge measure or
   234	  link integration appears; the class is background-free. Positive
   235	  routes weighed for the extension itself: (1) transfer the qubit
   236	  theorem through JW — ATTEMPTED and REFUTED as a route (string
   237	  exhibit, gated); (2) restrict to JW-adjacent bonds — ATTEMPTED as a
   238	  scoping and rejected (covers only one-dimensional orderings); (3)
   239	  hopping-only lemma — ATTEMPTED and widened (the `(−1)^{p·q}` law
   240	  covers all even monomials; pairing gate); (4) odd bond terms —
   241	  ATTEMPTED and EXCLUDED with the gated necessity exhibit; (5) odd
   242	  observables — ATTEMPTED and INCLUDED at the price of the explicit
   243	  zeroth term, gated.
   244	- **N2 hypothesis independence (pairwise).** Four supplied conditions;
   245	  each pair separates at a named proof step: evenness vs Hermiticity —
   246	  an odd Hermitian term (Majorana `c_x + c_x^†`) satisfies the second
   247	  and breaks the reduction (gate C5), while an even non-Hermitian term
   248	  (`c_x^† c_y`) satisfies the first and breaks `H̃^† = H̃`; evenness
   249	  vs `J` — the norm bound never enters the graded lemma, and evenness
   250	  never enters the majorization; evenness vs `d ≥ 1` — evenness kills
   251	  the zeroth term regardless of `d`, while `d` sets the series start
   252	  regardless of parity; Hermiticity vs `J` — `H̃^† = H̃` is used only
   253	  in the cited norm transport, `J` only in the walk majorization;
   254	  Hermiticity vs `d ≥ 1` and `J` vs `d ≥ 1` — disjoint proof
   255	  locations (norm transport / majorization vs series start). No
   256	  condition implies another.
   257	- **N3 hidden-wall scan.** New load-bearing content beyond the sibling
   258	  chain is exactly one lemma (the graded locality lemma) plus the
   259	  faithfulness of the JW realization (declared context, with the CAR
   260	  relations gated in the representation). No superselection, vacuum,

+# Microcausality: Weighted Quasilocal-Class Walk-Expansion Lieb-Robinson Bound
+
+**Date:** 2026-07-18
+**Type:** bounded_theorem
+**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
+**Scope:** bridge-conditional; supplied weighted quasilocal class
+(arbitrary-size connected supports, supplied `κ`); ambient `l1` graph
+metric declared; the axioms supply no dynamics; same conventions and
+declared ODE context as the siblings.
+**Audit-status authority:** independent audit lane only. This note sets
+no audit verdict and predicts none.
+**Primitive status:** no primitive is approved, registered, edited, or
+enlarged here.
+**Primary runner:**
+[`scripts/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py`](../scripts/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py)
+**Runner cache:**
+[`logs/runner-cache/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt)
+
+## Purpose, with the delta named up front
+
+The family's bounds so far require strict finite range (bonds; bonds
+and faces). The log-transfer generators the lane ultimately cares about
+are not finite-range — they are quasilocal, with exponentially decaying
+terms. The repo already carries three landed quasilocal results, and
+this note's value is only honest if its delta over them is stated
+plainly:
+
+- [`FREE_BILINEAR_QUASILOCAL_LR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md`](FREE_BILINEAR_QUASILOCAL_LR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md)
+  already proves the `U = 1` scalar-bilinear instance end-to-end: its
+  `W_mu := sup_x sum_y ||Phi_{xy}|| exp(mu d_1(x,y))` is precisely the
+  pair-support case of this note's `κ`, and its bound
+  `≤ 2||A_x||||B_y|| exp(−mu d_1(x,y) + 4 W_mu|t|)` is the pair-support
+  case of this note's display. **Not re-proved here** — needled as the
+  landed comparator instance.
+- [`EXP_DECAY_LIEB_ROBINSON_QUASILOCAL_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-11.md`](EXP_DECAY_LIEB_ROBINSON_QUASILOCAL_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-11.md)
+  proves a many-body LR bound in a polynomial-corrected weight, and —
+  crucially — a no-go: the pure-exponential **reproducing** constant is
+  unbounded (`≥ R + 1`). That no-go binds the reproducing-weight
+  METHOD. This note's route never forms that ratio (the decay factor is
+  extracted before any convolution), which is why a pure-exponential
+  weight is admissible here. Dispositioned in full below.
+- [`MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md)
+  supplies the finite-range chain-count skeleton this note upgrades,
+  and names the composition gap this note takes: "that one-step
+  composition theorem is not proved here or in the cited note."
+
+**The delta, precisely:** (i) supports of **arbitrary size** — the
+`|S|`-weighted activity `κ` with the peeling bookkeeping in which each
+step's `|S_j|` factor is consumed exactly to reconstitute the
+site-weighted summand of the next step (the landed notes are
+pair/overlap-based; this is the new counting content); (ii) the
+**family form** — all-time, volume-uniform, with explicit constants and
+the same Duhamel chain the siblings gate natively; (iii) the
+**fermionic instantiation** — the block04 sibling's graded lemma
+already covers long-range even elements, so its nearest-neighbor
+restriction lifts to the weighted class (re-gated on a long-range
+instance); (iv) the **disposition layer** connecting these three landed
+notes coherently. The gauged-kernel identification (the CT note's
+fixed-background kernel fed into `κ`) is **not attempted** — it is the
+named candidate next step.
+
+## Hypotheses (all supplied, none derived)
+
+A finite region `Λ ⊂ Z^3` with the ambient `l1` graph metric `d(·,·)`
+(declared once, used everywhere: `diam(S) := max_{u,v∈S} d(u,v)` is the
+**ambient** diameter). A supplied set-indexed interaction family: for
+finite **connected** `S ⊆ Λ`, Hermitian `h_S` (same-support terms
+summed into one `h_S` before norms are taken), `H = Σ_S h_S`, with the
+supplied weighted activity
+
+> `κ := sup_x Σ_{S∋x} ||h_S|| · |S| · e^{μ·diam(S)} < ∞`
+
+for a supplied `μ > 0`. The `|S|` weight is load-bearing: it is what
+absorbs the contact-site choice in the peeling below. Observables `A`,
+`B` on disjoint supports `X`, `Y` (`d = d(X,Y) ≥ 1`, the standing
+scoping hypothesis), on either the tensor-product class (arbitrary
+finite site dimensions, as in the plaquette sibling) or the even-CAR
+class (even Hermitian `h_S`; block04 conventions, including the
+explicit odd-odd zeroth term). Heisenberg convention, the declared
+finite-matrix ODE context, and directed time with the `H → −H`
+extension, all unchanged from the siblings; the Duhamel layer (Jacobi,
+boundary reduction with **consecutive** overlap — each iterate's
+reduced generator sums over supports meeting the *previous term only*,
+which is exactly the siblings' per-term re-derivation — self-drop, norm
+transport, iterated integrals `|t|^k/k!`) is cited to the siblings
+where natively gated and consumed here as the unrolled series. The
+axioms supply no dynamics (needled). No literature statement is
+load-bearing. Workhorse disclosure: this block used two Opus 4.8
+max-effort workers (scout and math build) under the workhorse skill's
+substitution clause; both were graded against supervisor derivations
+recorded in the loop pack **before** reading worker output, and every
+load-bearing fact below is gated natively in the runner.
+
+## Results
+
+**Chain lemma (rebuilt).** For a chain `(S_1, …, S_k)` with
+`S_1 ∩ X ≠ ∅`, `S_{j+1} ∩ S_j ≠ ∅`, `S_k ∩ Y ≠ ∅`: by induction every
+site of `S_j` lies within `Σ_{i≤j} diam(S_i)` of `X` (anchor in the
+overlap, triangle inequality, ambient diameter), so
+
+> `Σ_{j=1}^{k} diam(S_j) ≥ d`.
+
+Gated by enumeration on explicit mixed-size families. Hence the weight
+split: `Π_j ||h_{S_j}|| ≤ e^{−μd} · Π_j (||h_{S_j}|| e^{μ diam S_j})`.
+
+**Single-step meeting bound and the peeling (the `|S|` bookkeeping).**
+For a fixed `S`, summing the site-weighted value
+`w*(S') := ||h_{S'}|| |S'| e^{μ diam S'}` over all `S'` meeting `S`:
+
+> `Σ_{S'∩S≠∅} w*(S') ≤ Σ_{x∈S} Σ_{S'∋x} w*(S') ≤ |S| · κ`,
+
+with strict inequality whenever some `S'` meets `S` in two or more
+sites (the union bound counts it once per contact site — this exact
+slack is exhibited below on bonds: `12` versus the true `11`). The
+back-to-front peeling then bounds the full chain sum: the innermost
+tail obeys `U_k(S_{k−1}) ≤ |S_{k−1}| κ`, and at each outward step the
+`|S_j|` handed up combines with the plain weight
+`w(S_j) = ||h_{S_j}||e^{μ diam S_j}` to reconstitute exactly
+`w*(S_j) = |S_j| w(S_j)` — the object the meeting bound knows how to
+sum. No `|S|` factor is created or lost; by downward induction
+
+> `Σ_chains Π_j w(S_j) ≤ n_X^w · κ^{k−1} ≤ |X| · κ^k`,
+> `n_X^w := Σ_{S∩X≠∅} w*(S) ≤ |X|κ`,
+
+and with the weight split, the plain-norm chain sum obeys
+`Σ_chains Π_j ||h_{S_j}|| ≤ e^{−μd} n_X^w κ^{k−1}`. Gated two ways: the
+reconstitution identity symbolically, and the full chain sum versus the
+bound by exact enumeration on a finite mixed family.
+
+**Theorem (weighted quasilocal all-time volume-uniform Lieb-Robinson
+bound).** Feeding the counting layer into the siblings' unrolled series
+(prefactor `2||A||`, interior `2^{k−1}`, base `2||h_{S_k}||||B||` —
+total `2^{k+1}` at order `k`, cross-checked by two groupings) and
+resumming exactly (`2^{k+1}κ^{k−1}|t|^k = (2/κ)(2κ|t|)^k`):
+
+> `||[τ_t(A), B]|| ≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X^w/κ) · e^{−μd} ·
+> (e^{2κ|t|} − 1)`
+> `≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| |X| · e^{−μd} · (e^{2κ|t|} − 1)`,
+
+for all `t` and every finite `Λ`, with constants depending only on
+`||A||`, `||B||`, `n_X^w ≤ |X|κ`, `κ`, `μ`, `d` — not on `|Λ|` and not
+on the site dimensions. `||[A, B]|| = 0` on the tensor class and on the
+even sector of the CAR class; for odd-odd CAR pairs the zeroth term is
+kept exactly as in the block04 sibling. Sanity, gated: at `t = 0` the
+bound is tight (`e^0 − 1 = 0`); the `k = 1` series coefficient matches
+the direct order-1 term. Velocity readout: `v ≤ 2κ/μ` — **not claimed
+sharp** (see the slack ladder below).
+
+**Consistency reduction and the slack ladder (exact, enumerated).** On
+the strict bond class (`||h_b|| ≤ J`): `κ = 12Je^μ` exactly (`6` bonds
+per site × `|b| = 2` × `e^μ`), so the rate is `24Je^μ` versus the
+direct sibling's `20Je^μ` — **weaker by exactly `6/5`**, same shape.
+The slack decomposes exactly and is gated by enumeration: the meeting
+bound's union count gives `12` where the true count of distinct bonds
+meeting a bond is `11` (self double-counted at both endpoints), and the
+sibling's Duhamel self-drop removes the self term for `10`. The direct
+finite-range siblings remain sharper on their classes and are not
+modified; this theorem's value is the class, not the constant.
+
+**Instance families with closed-form `κ` (exact).** Pair interactions
+`h_{x,y} = J_0 λ^{|x−y|} K` (`K` a fixed two-site Hermitian of norm 1,
+`λ` rational, `ρ := λe^μ < 1`): with the `l1` sphere count on `Z^3`
+`N_3(r) = 4r^2 + 2` (gated by enumeration at two radii),
+
+> `κ_3D = 4J_0 · ρ(3 + ρ^2)/(1 − ρ)^3`,  `κ_1D = 4J_0 · ρ/(1 − ρ)`,
+
+via the geometric identities `Σ r ρ^r = ρ/(1−ρ)^2`,
+`Σ r^2 ρ^r = ρ(1+ρ)/(1−ρ)^3` and the exact numerator algebra
+`4ρ(1+ρ) + 2ρ(1−ρ)^2 = 2ρ(3+ρ^2)` (gated symbolically); rational
+instances gated by partial-sum-plus-tail brackets: `κ_3D = 14J_0` at
+`ρ = 1/3`, `κ_3D = 684J_0` at `ρ = 3/4`, `κ_1D = 4J_0` at `ρ = 1/2`.
+The landed free-bilinear note's `W_mu` bound is precisely the
+pair-support instance of this shape at `U = 1` — needled as landed,
+not re-proved.
+
+**Fermionic lift (nearest-neighbor restriction removed).** The block04
+sibling's graded locality lemma is support-size-blind: even elements of
+disjoint support commute regardless of range. Hence the boundary
+reduction and base-term vanishing hold verbatim for weighted long-range
+even families, and the theorem's CAR form follows with no new algebra.
+Re-gated on a long-range instance: the distant even pair term
+`c_0^† c_3 + c_3^† c_0` commutes exactly with the intermediate odd
+generators and even density, and the weighted reduction
+`[H, n_0] = [Σ_{S∋0} h_S, n_0]` holds on a mixed NN-plus-long-range
+family.
+
+**Disposition of the pure-exponential no-go (load-bearing).** The
+exp-decay note proves: the reproducing ratio
+`Σ_z G_μ(d(x,z))G_μ(d(z,y))/G_μ(d(x,y)) ≥ R + 1` — pure-exponential
+weights have no finite reproducing constant, and its own many-body
+bound therefore uses a polynomial-corrected weight. **That obstruction
+does not bind this note's route:** here the decay `e^{−μd}` is
+extracted from each chain **before** any summation over intermediate
+supports (the weight split), and the peeling sums only the site-weighted
+activity per step — the reproducing ratio is never formed. The
+free-bilinear note's landed pure-exponential bound is the existing
+instance of exactly this move. This disposition is the reason a
+pure-exponential `|S|`-weighted hypothesis is admissible where the
+reproducing method fails; both of that note's relevant displays are
+needled.
+

# Review Feedback Workflow

**Purpose:** define the canonical process for landing, reviewing, triaging, and
closing repo feedback on `main`

## Canonical Files

- active queue:
  [`ACTIVE_REVIEW_QUEUE.md`](./ACTIVE_REVIEW_QUEUE.md)
- historical detailed packets:
  [`docs/work_history/repo/review_feedback/README.md`](../work_history/repo/review_feedback/README.md)
- historical planning backlogs:
  [`docs/work_history/repo/backlog/README.md`](../work_history/repo/backlog/README.md)

## Default Process

1. Land the candidate work on `main` if it is already honest enough to keep.
2. Have the reviewer check the landed surface or the clean science-only review
   branch.
3. Record any actionable finding in
   [`ACTIVE_REVIEW_QUEUE.md`](./ACTIVE_REVIEW_QUEUE.md).
4. If the review needs more than a short bullet list, add a detailed packet in
   [`docs/work_history/repo/review_feedback/`](../work_history/repo/review_feedback/README.md)
   and link it from the queue.
5. Triage each item into one of five buckets:
   - `fix on main`
   - `support-only demotion`
   - `science-needed`
   - `reject`
   - `historical only`
6. Resolve the item on `main` if it is wording, packaging, code, or honest
   demotion work.
7. Remove the item from the active queue once the repo-facing state is correct.

## Decision Rule

Use the narrowest honest fix:

- if the issue is wording, packaging, stale status language, or a reproducible
  code bug, fix it on `main`
- if the issue is a real missing theorem step or unjustified selector, do not
  fake closure; either demote the claim or keep the science off-main until the
  derivation exists
- if the issue concerns a historical lane that is no longer part of the live
  evidence chain, classify it as `historical only` rather than treating it as a
  live blocker

## Placement Rule

- do **not** put new review packets in the front-door `docs/` root unless they
  are themselves part of the live science package
- do **not** create new free-floating backlog files for current review work
- do **not** use branch-local notes as the long-term review source of truth

## What Belongs Where

- `docs/repo/ACTIVE_REVIEW_QUEUE.md`
  current actionable review state
- `docs/work_history/repo/review_feedback/`
  older audit notes, detailed review packets, and resolved review histories
- `docs/work_history/repo/backlog/`
  planning/backlog notes that are not current review truth surfaces

# Controlled Vocabulary

> **Front-door lookup:** Looking up a single term? Go to
> `docs/KEY_TERMINOLOGY.md` — flat A-Z index of
> every repo-canonical term with one-line definition and a pointer to
> the source-of-truth doc for each.

## Vocabulary Hierarchy

The repo has five language layers. This doc is the **operational** layer
(layer 3). Higher layers are sovereign for their domains; do not duplicate
their content here, and do not override their wording from here.

| Layer | Source-of-truth doc | Governs |
|---|---|---|
| 0. Front-door A-Z lookup | `docs/KEY_TERMINOLOGY.md` | Single-page A-Z index of every repo-canonical term with a one-line definition and a pointer to its source-of-truth doc. The reader entry-point above all layers below; an index, not an authority. New terms enter the index only after the source-of-truth doc accepts them. |
| 1. Framework substantive | `docs/MINIMAL_AXIOMS_2026-06-29.md` and `docs/audit/data/axiom_premise_nodes.json` | Framework axioms and explicitly approved primitive premise nodes the operational vocabulary uses: Lattice (`Z^3` with nearest-neighbor adjacency, standard translations, proper cubic rotations about each site, no privileged site, and site distinctions carried only by the supplied lattice structure), Qubit (the domain of local possibilities with full one-site algebraic presentation `M_2(ℂ)`; `Cl(3,0)` is equivalent notation, not extra primitive content; no possibility is privileged; possibilities are distinguished by the supplied algebraic structure alone), Admissibility (one fixed nearest-neighbor rule, covariant under lattice translations and proper cubic rotations, by which the available possibilities are determined by, and vary with, the nearest-neighbor conditions at each site), Record (when present, fixed locking of exactly one admissible local possibility; at most one record per site; permanence; readout value determined by record content alone; finite scalar additivity), the state/law qualification clauses, the definition of `A_min`, the status of prior `A3` / `A4` / `A5` as open gates rather than axioms, and registered primitive premise nodes such as the scale-reference and kinetic-isotropy primitives. Changes only when a framework-level science decision changes. |
| 2. External paper text | [`docs/ai_methodology/CANONICAL_FRAMING_PARAGRAPH_2026-04-25.md`](../ai_methodology/CANONICAL_FRAMING_PARAGRAPH_2026-04-25.md), [`docs/ai_methodology/AI_ACCOUNTABILITY_AND_DISCLOSURE_NOTE_2026-04-25.md`](../ai_methodology/AI_ACCOUNTABILITY_AND_DISCLOSURE_NOTE_2026-04-25.md) | Verbatim reusable disclosure paragraphs for papers, preprints, and talks. The framing paragraph is the short paper-facing form; the accountability disclosure is the longer package-level form. Both carry their own usage guidance (e.g. replacing `[repo URL]`, narrowing the tool list when only one was used). |
| 3. Operational (this doc) | `docs/repo/CONTROLLED_VOCABULARY.md` | Status taxonomy, claim-strength labels, audit-lane field enums, repair classes, filename conventions, archival paths, topic language, and paper-facing prose voice — the working vocabulary used inside the repo across ledgers, tables, notes, runners, skills, and PR descriptions. |
| 4. Methodology framing (adjacent) | `docs/AI_METHODOLOGY_NOTE_2026-04-25.md` | The curated front-door note for the methodology lane. Defines how to talk about the AI / methodology side at the project level. |

For a single-term lookup (layer 0), use `KEY_TERMINOLOGY.md`. For
framework axioms and approved primitive premise nodes (layer 1), cite
`MINIMAL_AXIOMS_2026-06-29.md` and
`docs/audit/data/axiom_premise_nodes.json` — do not redefine Lattice, Qubit,
Admissibility, Record, `Cl(3)`, `Z^3`, `A_min`, or registered primitives here. For external paper text
(layer 2), use the canonical framing paragraph and the accountability
disclosure verbatim — do not paraphrase. This doc governs layer 3 only.

---

This file is the single repo-wide home for the **operational** vocabulary
layer: status taxonomy, audit-lane field enums, filename conventions,
axiom-naming **rules** (above the science decision in layer 1), archival
paths, topic language, and paper-facing prose voice. Other policy /
process docs in the repo cite this file for vocab rather than inlining
their own. If a sanctioned term, label, suffix, or wording is not listed
here and is not defined in the layer 1 or layer 2 sources above, it does
not exist for repo purposes — vocabulary additions require a PR against
this file before any note, runner, ledger row, status badge, or PR
description that uses them.

Goals:

- one taxonomy home for matrix / ledger / atlas / review / note / runner / script
  language
- separate publication-capture decisions from theorem-strength labels
- separate authoring vocabulary from audit-lane field enums
- avoid vague shorthand on live package surfaces
- prefer explicit protocol qualifiers over loose adjectives
- forbid new tags / classes / meta-framings on `claim_type: meta` notes
  and elsewhere; emergent vocabulary is a substantive defect even when
  the per-claim content is correct

## Vocabulary Families

There are five status families in this repo. Do not mix them casually.

1. publication-capture dispositions
2. claim-strength / release labels
3. audit-lane field vocabulary (claim_type / audit_status /
   effective_status / repair classes / independence tiers)
4. historical lane-board labels
5. historical discovery-log labels

If a file already has separate columns for `Status`, `Qualifier`, `Import
class`, or `Current publication decision`, keep one family per column
rather than collapsing everything into one hybrid phrase.

## Science Naming Rules

Landed science must use domain-explicit names. Do not introduce a bare
letter-number or route code as the primary name of a theorem, lane, source
note, runner headline, table row, or review finding when that code could also
mean an axiom, assumption, algebra type, branch block, route number, or
workstream index.

Examples of ambiguous primary names to avoid on new landed surfaces:

- `A1`, `A2`, `G1`, `R3`
- `Route F`, `Block 2`, `Origin B`
- `Step 1` or `Assumption 1` compressed to `S1` / `A1`

Use the scientific object as the name instead:

- `physical Cl(3) local algebra`, not bare `A1`
- `Lattice`, not bare `A2`
- `Koide Frobenius-equipartition condition`, not bare `A1`
- `Lie type A_1` or `SU(2) root-system check`, not bare `A1`
- `DM-eta Coleman-Weinberg residual`, not bare `G1`

**Canonical mathematical-object names are allowed.** Bare letter-number
forms that name an *established mathematical object* — `S^3` (3-sphere),
`S^2` (2-sphere), `U(1)` and `SU(N)` (gauge groups), `Z_3` / `Z_n`
(cyclic groups), `C_3` (cyclic / symmetry group), `A_n` / `D_n` /
`E_n` (Lie types), `I_3` (third isospin component), etc. — are the
canonical names for those objects and do not require domain-explicit
expansion. The rule above targets *code-like primary names that don't
reference an established mathematical object* (e.g. `A1` used as a
workstream label, `G1` used as a route code, `R3` used as a routing
index), not standard mathematical notation. If both readings are
plausible in context (e.g. `A_1` as Lie type vs. `A1` as
workstream-index abbreviation), spell out the mathematical reading
on first use (`Lie type A_1`) so the bare form is unambiguous.

Legacy shorthand may be preserved only as an alias after the explicit name,
for example: `Koide Frobenius-equipartition condition (legacy alias: A1)`.
If an existing file already uses a shorthand, new edits should define it on
first use and prefer the explicit name in headings, metadata, claim scopes,
review summaries, and publication/control-plane rows.

Axioms and assumptions must be written out as `Axiom 1`, `Assumption 1`, or a
descriptive premise name. Do not abbreviate them to `A1` on live science
surfaces.

### Meta-note vocabulary discipline

`claim_type: meta` notes (audit-prep, synthesis, fix-record, infrastructure
documentation) must use a plain `_NOTE_<YYYY-MM-DD>.md` filename suffix
and repo-canonical vocabulary in their content. The following emergent
patterns are vocabulary additions and forbidden until they appear in this
file:

- new filename suffixes such as `_HOSTILE_AUDIT_FINDINGS_NOTE_`,
  `_DOWNSTREAM_FIX_NOTE_`, `_SURGICAL_FIX_RECORD_`, `_FINDINGS_MEMO_`,
  `_FIX_RECORD_`, `_AUDIT_BRIEF_`, `_ADDENDUM_`
- new within-note labelling schemes such as `F-A` / `F-B` / `F-C`
  "Finding F-letter" headings
- new finding-class names such as `framing-fix`, `routing correction`,
  `tier over-claim`, `admission-inheritance`, `audit-prep input`
- new authority-role phrases such as
  `Status authority: independent audit lane only` as a load-bearing role
- new repair-class names beyond the seven listed in "Audit Lane Field
  Vocabulary" below
- new "X is a Y" meta-framings that imply a class or parent-framing
  cross-reference for a collection of not-yet-shipped sub-PRs

The reviewer rejects custom tags / classes / meta-framings even when the
underlying per-claim work is correct. Ship narrow content one identity at a
time, mirroring an existing canonical template, with no new vocabulary.

## Filename Taxonomy

### Script filename prefixes

- `frontier_*`
  - active frontier or later-stage retained runners
  - this is the current default namespace
- `mirror_*`, `lattice_*`, `gate_b_*`, `source_resolved_*`, `wave_*`
  - older major programs that are still important on `main`
- everything else
  - usually lane-specific historical or exploratory runners

Lane-specific prefixes are preferred when creating new frontier runners:
`frontier_staggered_*`, `frontier_two_field_*`, `frontier_emergent_geometry_*`,
etc. Pick a prefix that names the lane, not the date or the author.

### Note filename rules

- New retained notes should be explicit about the runner they interpret
  in the filename when feasible.
- New strategy or synthesis notes should not silently replace retained
  notes; if the synthesis is forward-looking it lands at
  `claim_type: meta` with the `_NOTE_<YYYY-MM-DD>.md` plain suffix.
- Historical lanes should not be renamed just to fit the current
  taxonomy. They should be indexed in the lane board and registry
  instead. If a lane is historical, mark that in the lane board and
  registry rather than renaming dozens of files.

### Reserved filename suffixes

See "Meta-note vocabulary discipline" above for the list of forbidden
emergent suffixes. The only sanctioned form for new audit-prep / meta
notes is `<DOMAIN>_NOTE_<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`.

## Publication-Capture Dispositions

Use these on publication-control-plane surfaces such as
`PUBLICATION_MATRIX.md`.

| Label | Use |
|---|---|
| `retained` | live retained family on the current paper-authority surface |
| `promoted` | main-paper publication-core family carried directly in the current paper package |
| `bounded` | live captured family kept outside the main paper core with explicit caveats |
| `open` | live gate / blocker that is not yet closed |
| `frozen-out` | intentionally excluded from the main paper while still recorded |

These are package-capture decisions, not generic adjectives.

## Claim-Strength / Release Labels

Use these on notes, claims tables, quantitative tables, and runner summaries.

| Label | Use |
|---|---|
| `retained` | theorem-grade closure on the retained authority surface |
| `derived` | current-main downstream result obtained from retained structure plus named bridge/import assumptions; safe to quote, but not the same as zero-input retained closure |
| `retained corollary` | safe retained consequence of a retained theorem/action surface |
| `retained support theorem` / `retained support` | exact retained support result that is reusable but not itself the manuscript headline |
| `retained support batch` | coherent retained batch of support theorems/tools carried together for atlas, reviewer, or support-package reuse |
| `retained exact theorem` / `retained exact companion` | exact retained theorem/companion variant used when exactness itself is part of the safe statement |
| `retained structural theorem` / `retained exact structural theorem` | retained exact structural law on the framework/package surface |
| `retained support tool` | reusable retained tool/subderivation that supports other lanes without itself being the manuscript headline |
| `retained framework statement` / `retained positioning` / `retained quantitative lane` | retained package-authority framing labels for front-door/control-plane use |
| `retained action-surface closure` / `retained restricted theorem` / `retained restricted support` / `retained evaluated theorem` | retained exact-result variants where the constrained surface/class/evaluation matters |
| `retained positive` / `retained partial positive` | retained positive lane result used mainly on atlas/toolbox surfaces rather than as the headline theorem |
| `exact support theorem` / `exact support` | accepted exact-support variant when exactness itself is material to the safe statement; use with the same non-headline semantics as retained support, and keep bridge/import qualifiers explicit |
| `exact support batch` | coherent exact batch of support theorems/tools carried together for atlas or reviewer reuse |
| `exact structural theorem` / `exact algebraic identity` / `exact subderivation` | accepted exact-result specializations for atlas / theorem-bank rows when the mathematical role matters |
| `exact boundary theorem` / `exact reduction theorem` | accepted exact-result specializations for atlas / theorem-bank rows when the mathematical role is an open-lane boundary or a compression/reduction statement |
| `exact current-stack theorem` / `exact current-bank theorem` / `exact current-bank reduction` / `exact current-bank no-go theorem` | accepted exact-result specializations for current-stack/current-bank closeout rows on atlas and reviewer surfaces |
| `exact negative boundary` / `exact negative closeout` / `exact post-selector reduction` | accepted exact-result specializations for explicit no-go/closeout/post-selector roles on atlas and reviewer surfaces |
| `exact support/boundary theorem` | accepted mixed exact-role label when a row simultaneously contributes a reusable exact support input and an exact open-lane boundary statement |
| `exact support tool` | reusable exact tool/subderivation that supports other lanes without being the headline claim |
| `bounded support theorem` / `bounded support note` / `bounded support tool` | bounded supporting result/tool carried for atlas, reviewer, or secondary-lane use; not a main closure claim |
| `bounded support batch` | coherent bounded batch of support tools/calculations carried for atlas or reviewer reuse |
| `promoted quantitative package` | quantitative package strong enough for the current main-paper surface |
| `bounded companion` | live bounded supporting lane outside the main paper core |
| `bounded secondary lane` | live bounded lane worth carrying, but clearly secondary to the main package |
| `bounded frontier` / `bounded negative boundary` / `bounded Route 2 build candidate` | bounded frontier/result classes kept for ongoing design work rather than current-package promotion |
| `main closure package` | package-level main-paper closure claim on the current review/package surface; stronger than support, but not automatically the retained quantitative paper core |
| `conditional` / `support` | useful positive package whose load-bearing step is still conditional, imposed, or support-only |
| `open main gate` | still-open main closure target |
| `historical` / `diagnostic` | preserved for audit/history/instrumentation, not live evidence |
| `historical support` / `provenance` / `exact transport provenance` | provenance-only rows kept for route history, reviewer handoff, or closure bookkeeping rather than live promotion |
| `negative-result` | useful negative or no-go result |
| `negative-result` / `support` | negative/no-go result that is also a reusable support/pruning surface |
| `inconclusive` | signal exists but interpretation is not frozen |

Allowed composite forms should be built from the labels above and kept narrow:

- `retained exact theorem`
- `retained exact companion`
- `retained structural theorem`
- `retained exact structural theorem`
- `retained corollary`
- `retained support theorem`
- `retained support batch`
- `retained support tool`
- `exact support theorem`
- `exact support batch`
- `exact structural theorem`
- `exact algebraic identity`
- `exact subderivation`
- `exact boundary theorem`
- `exact reduction theorem`
- `exact current-stack theorem`
- `exact current-bank theorem`
- `exact current-bank reduction`
- `exact current-bank no-go theorem`
- `exact negative boundary`
- `exact negative closeout`
- `exact post-selector reduction`
- `exact support/boundary theorem`
- `exact support tool`
- `bounded support theorem`
- `bounded support note`
- `bounded support tool`
# Primitive Registry Check

Use this check before classifying any dependency, premise, import, wall,
conditional input, or bounded-status source.

1. Read `docs/audit/data/axiom_premise_nodes.json`.
2. For each registered primitive node relevant to the claim, read the node's
   `current_path` source note. Do not rely on memory or prose elsewhere.
3. Treat registered primitives as approved premise nodes. They chain-satisfy
   dependencies without making downstream rows `retained_bounded`.
4. Do not classify a registered primitive as an axiom,
   imported value, missing premise, no-go wall, or source of bounded status.
5. Do not grant more than the primitive source note declares. Any
   dimensionless quantity, selector, weighting rule, normalization rule,
   probability rule, readout bridge, dynamics, source/action, or empirical
   match remains separate unless independently derived.
6. Treat any proposed primitive absent from the registry as unapproved. It
   requires explicit owner approval and a reviewed registry/policy update
   before a worker may use it as an accepted premise.

Current approved primitives:

- `scale_reference_primitive`:
  `docs/SCALE_REFERENCE_PRIMITIVE_NOTE.md`. This grants the single
  dimensionful scale reference `a^{-1} = M_Pl` as a units conversion only.
  It is not a Planck import and not a bounded-status
  source. It does not assert `a/l_P = 1` as a derived theorem or supply any
  dimensionless physics.
- `kinetic_isotropy_primitive`:
  `docs/KINETIC_ISOTROPY_PRIMITIVE_NOTE_2026-06-09.md`. This grants only the
  structural OS0 kinetic-form isotropy `c_t = c_s`: the emergent tick is
  grained on the same footing as the spatial edge. It is not an axiom and not
  a bounded-status source. It does not supply an
  absolute scale, spacing-ratio theorem, dynamics, Lorentz-closure theorem,
  mass ratio, coupling, mixing angle, phase, selector, readout bridge,
  probability rule, normalization rule, or empirical match.
- `realized_state_primitive`:
  `docs/REALIZED_STATE_PRIMITIVE_NOTE_2026-06-11.md`. This grants only
  pointwise evaluation at a supplied law-admissible realized state
  (the axioms select no state; a history fixes one). It is not an axiom and
  not a bounded-status source. It does not supply a
  state, state-selection rule, measure, typicality or genericity assumption,
  weighting, normalization, probability rule, preferred or default state, or
  any state-contingent value; a quoted number that would differ had another
  permitted state been realized is registered data, not derivation output.
  The past hypothesis is explicitly not housed by this primitive.

# Active Review Queue

**Status:** canonical live queue for current-main review feedback  
**Purpose:** single place to record reviewer findings that still need a decision,
fix, or explicit rejection on `main`

## Rule

Use this file for **active** review feedback only.

- add new reviewer findings here first
- keep each item short and decision-oriented
- link any long-form packet in
  [`docs/work_history/repo/review_feedback/`](../work_history/repo/review_feedback/README.md)
- when an item is resolved, remove it from the open list and record it in the
  queue history section or the linked detailed packet

Do not use scattered backlog notes or branch-local memos as the live review
truth surface.

## Intake Format

Record each new finding as one bullet:

- `ID`
  short label; date if needed
- `Scope`
  affected lane, note, script, or package surface
- `Finding`
  one-sentence statement of the issue
- `Disposition`
  one of: `triage`, `fix on main`, `support-only demotion`, `science-needed`,
  `reject`
- `Detail`
  optional link to a longer packet in work history
